<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mozambique&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Mozambique" title="Culture of Mozambique">Culture of Mozambique</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mozambique_-_mask.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a0/Mozambique_-_mask.jpg/170px-Mozambique_-_mask.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="252" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a0/Mozambique_-_mask.jpg/255px-Mozambique_-_mask.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a0/Mozambique_-_mask.jpg/340px-Mozambique_-_mask.jpg 2x" data-file-width="720" data-file-height="1067" /></a><figcaption>Woman with traditional mask in Mozambique</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Museu_Da_Ilha_De_Mocambique_(33387901932).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Museu_Da_Ilha_De_Mocambique_%2833387901932%29.jpg/220px-Museu_Da_Ilha_De_Mocambique_%2833387901932%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Museu_Da_Ilha_De_Mocambique_%2833387901932%29.jpg/330px-Museu_Da_Ilha_De_Mocambique_%2833387901932%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Museu_Da_Ilha_De_Mocambique_%2833387901932%29.jpg/440px-Museu_Da_Ilha_De_Mocambique_%2833387901932%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Island_of_Mozambique" title="Island of Mozambique">Island of Mozambique</a>, 2016</figcaption></figure><p>Mozambique was ruled by Portugal, and they share a main language (Portuguese) and main religion (Roman Catholicism). But since most of the people of Mozambique are Bantus, most of the culture is native; for Bantus living in urban areas, there is some Portuguese influence. Mozambican culture also influences the <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Portugal" title="Culture of Portugal">Portuguese culture</a>.
</p><h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Arts">Arts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mozambique&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Arts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Makonde are known for their wood carving and elaborate masks, which are commonly used in traditional dances. There are two different kinds of wood carvings: <a href="/wiki/Shetani" title="Shetani">shetani</a>, (evil spirits), which are mostly carved in heavy <a href="/wiki/Ebony" title="Ebony">ebony</a>, tall, and elegantly curved with symbols and nonrepresentational faces; and <a href="/wiki/Ujamaa" title="Ujamaa">ujamaa</a>, which are totem-type carvings which illustrate lifelike faces of people and various figures. These sculptures are usually referred to as "family trees" because they tell stories of many generations.
</p><p>During the last years of the colonial period, Mozambican art reflected the oppression by the colonial power and became a symbol of resistance. After independence in 1975, modern art came into a new phase. The two best known and most influential contemporary Mozambican artists are the painter <a href="/wiki/Malangatana_Ngwenya" title="Malangatana Ngwenya">Malangatana Ngwenya</a> and the sculptor <a href="/wiki/Alberto_Chissano" title="Alberto Chissano">Alberto Chissano</a>. A lot of the post-independence art during the 1980s and 1990s reflect the political struggle, civil war, suffering, starvation, and struggle.
</p><p>Dances are usually intricate, highly developed traditions throughout Mozambique. There are many different kinds of dances from tribe to tribe which are usually ritualistic in nature. The Chopi, for instance, act out battles dressed in animal skins. The men of Makua dress in colourful outfits and masks while dancing on stilts around the village for hours. Groups of women in the northern part of the country perform a traditional dance called <a href="/wiki/Tufo_(dance)" title="Tufo (dance)"><i>tufo</i></a>, to celebrate Islamic holidays.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mozambique&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_cuisine" title="Portuguese cuisine">Portuguese cuisine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cuisine_of_Mozambique" class="mw-redirect" title="Cuisine of Mozambique">Cuisine of Mozambique</a>, and <a href="/wiki/African_cuisine" title="African cuisine">African cuisine</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"><table class="box-Unreferenced_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Mozambique&amp;action=edit">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">January 2022</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>With a nearly 500-year presence in the country, the Portuguese have greatly influenced Mozambique's cuisine. Staples and crops such as <a href="/wiki/Cassava" title="Cassava">cassava</a> (a starchy root of Brazilian origin) and <a href="/wiki/Cashew" title="Cashew">cashew</a> nuts (also of Brazilian origin, though Mozambique was once the largest producer of these nuts), and <i>pãozinho</i> (pronounced <span style="font-size:85%;"></span><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="pt-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Portuguese" title="Help:IPA/Portuguese">[pɐ̃wˈzĩɲu]</a></span>, Portuguese-style buns), were brought in by the Portuguese. The use of spices and seasonings such as <a href="/wiki/Bay_leaf" title="Bay leaf">bay leaves</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chili_pepper" title="Chili pepper">chili peppers</a>, fresh <a href="/wiki/Coriander" title="Coriander">coriander</a>, garlic, onions, <a href="/wiki/Paprika" title="Paprika">paprika</a>, red sweet peppers, and wine were introduced by the Portuguese, as were maize, <a href="/wiki/Millet" title="Millet">millet</a>, potatoes, rice, <a href="/wiki/Sorghum" title="Sorghum">sorghum</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sugarcane" title="Sugarcane">sugarcane</a>. <i><a href="/wiki/Espetada" title="Espetada">espetada</a></i>, the popular <i>inteiro com piripiri</i> (whole chicken in <a href="/wiki/Peri-peri" title="Peri-peri">piri-piri</a> sauce), <i>prego</i> (steak roll), <i>pudim</i> (pudding), and <i>rissóis</i> (battered shrimp) are all Portuguese dishes commonly eaten in present-day Mozambique.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mozambique&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Radio_Mo%C3%A7ambique_(4106248049).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Radio_Mo%C3%A7ambique_%284106248049%29.jpg/220px-Radio_Mo%C3%A7ambique_%284106248049%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Radio_Mo%C3%A7ambique_%284106248049%29.jpg/330px-Radio_Mo%C3%A7ambique_%284106248049%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Radio_Mo%C3%A7ambique_%284106248049%29.jpg/440px-Radio_Mo%C3%A7ambique_%284106248049%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="682" /></a><figcaption>Headquarters of Rádio Moçambique in <a href="/wiki/KaMpfumo" title="KaMpfumo">KaMpfumo</a> district of <a href="/wiki/Maputo" title="Maputo">Maputo</a> (photo 2009)</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Mass_media_in_Mozambique" title="Mass media in Mozambique">Mozambican media</a> is heavily influenced by the government.<sup id="cite_ref-salgado_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-salgado-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Newspapers have relatively low circulation rates because of high newspaper prices and low <a href="/wiki/Literacy_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Literacy rate">literacy rates</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-salgado_140-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-salgado-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Among the most highly circulated newspapers are state-controlled dailies, such as <i>Noticias</i> and <i>Diário de Moçambique</i>, and the weekly <i>Domingo</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-eisa_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-eisa-141">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> Their circulation is mostly confined to Maputo.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> Most funding and advertising revenue is given to pro-government newspapers.<sup id="cite_ref-salgado_140-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-salgado-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Radio programmes are the most influential form of media in the country because of ease of access.<sup id="cite_ref-salgado_140-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-salgado-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> State-owned radio stations are more popular than privately owned media. This is exemplified by the government radio station, Rádio Moçambique, the most popular station in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-salgado_140-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-salgado-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> It was established shortly after Mozambique's independence.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> The television stations watched by Mozambicans are STV, TIM, and TVM Televisão Moçambique. Through cable and satellite, viewers can access tens of other African, Asian, Brazilian, and European channels.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mozambique&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"><table class="box-Unreferenced_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Mozambique&amp;action=edit">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">May 2016</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Mozambique" title="Music of Mozambique">music of Mozambique</a> serves many purposes, ranging from religious expression to traditional ceremonies. Musical instruments are usually handmade. Some of the instruments used in Mozambican musical expression include drums made of wood and animal skin; the <i>lupembe</i>, a woodwind instrument made from animal horns or wood; and the <a href="/wiki/Marimba" title="Marimba">marimba</a>, which is a kind of xylophone native to Mozambique and other parts of Africa. The marimba is a popular instrument with the Chopi of the south-central coast, who are famous for their musical skill and dance.
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mozambique&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Football</a> (<a href="/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language">Portuguese</a>: <i lang="pt">futebol</i>) is the most popular sport in Mozambique. The national team is the <a href="/wiki/Mozambique_national_football_team" title="Mozambique national football team">Mozambique national football team</a>. Track and field and basketball are also avidly followed in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Roller_hockey_(quad)" title="Roller hockey (quad)">Roller hockey</a> is popular, and the best result for the national team was when they came in fourth at the <a href="/wiki/2011_FIRS_Men%27s_Roller_Hockey_World_Cup" title="2011 FIRS Men&#39;s Roller Hockey World Cup">2011 FIRS Roller Hockey World Cup</a>. The women's beach volleyball team finished 2nd at the <a href="/wiki/2018%E2%80%932020_CAVB_Beach_Volleyball_Continental_Cup" title="2018–2020 CAVB Beach Volleyball Continental Cup">2018–2020 CAVB Beach Volleyball Continental Cup</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Mozambique_national_cricket_team" title="Mozambique national cricket team">Mozambique national cricket team</a> represents the nation in <a href="/wiki/International_cricket" title="International cricket">international cricket</a>.
</p>
